| Декуплинг при совпадающих метках исходных элементов и полостных столбцов | **Доказан условно на G** для каждого фиксированного внедиагонального массива с ограниченными тестами; диагональ и сумма по растущему числу меток не включены |
| Полный хааровский закон массива связи | **Вычислен точно**: $Q$ независимо от $C$, а столбцы $C$ условно гауссовские с общей случайной обратной Wishart-ковариацией |
| Почти наверное детерминированная изотропия предельного полостного оператора | **Не выполнена даже для Haar**; изотропно его условное среднее при фиксированном выходе |
| Удаление совпадающей метки для приближения столбца связи | **Имеет немалую точную ошибку**: при открытии $k$ дополнительных каналов средний квадрат разности равен $k/(m+k)$; связи не должны разрываться |
| Совместимость всех степеней при смене внешнего порта | **Доказана точно**: $\mathcal L_r(dp)=r^2p_rM(dp)$, $\int p_r\,dM=r^{-2}$, $\int(1-p_0)\,dM=\zeta(2)$ |
| Полное хааровское распределение первой частоты при фиксированной нулевой частоте | **Вычислено точно**: под $\mathcal L_1$ имеем $p_0\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]$ и $p_1\mid p_0=t\sim\mathrm{Unif}[t(1-t),1-t]$ |
| Тензорная структура симплектической семьи | **Вычислена точно**: сопряжение с перестановкой тензорных сомножителей, дополнительная унитарность после realignment и сеть циркулянтных матриц; преобразование меняет физические порты |
| Одностороннее усреднение матрицы реакции Коши | **Доказано точно**, включая все положительные квадратичные источники; эти средние не определяют совместный закон матрицы реакции или пропускания |
| Смешанное усреднение одного Cauchy-параметра | **Вычислена ограниченная формула дисперсии рассеяния**; возможная сингулярность скалярного фактора компенсируется связью с каналами, а совместный закон направления остаётся неизвестным |
| Определение закона по всем совместным односторонним Cauchy-пробам после внешних смешиваний | **Исключено как метод идентификации**: эти пробы одинаковы при любом законе пропускания после добавления общей фазы |
| Определение первого момента бесконечной Gram-положительностью и одномерной inner-степенью | **Опровергнуто** явным законом (BG7), среднее которого пробегает весь интервал от нуля до единицы; это не согласованный контрпример с G |
| Одночастичные каналы симплектической семьи и классическая цепь в новой базе | **Доказаны двухшаговые сжатия** порядка $\sqrt{\log q/q}$ по вероятности; при этом глобальное тождество следов сохраняется и вывод о двух портах не установлен |
| Непрерывные тензорные деформации симплектической конструкции | **Проверены точно**: $\|B\|=[(1+\lvert\cos t_d\rvert)/2]^d$; при $t_d=c/\sqrt d$ предел равен $e^{-c^2/4}<1$, а предельный закон $T$ не вычислен |
| Полный спектр бесконечного Gram-оператора | **Классифицирован точно**: положительность эквивалентна неотрицательности всех множителей (SG10); неравномерные плотности (SG17)–(SG19) показывают предел этого отдельного метода |
| Почти наверное конечная норма Гильберта–Шмидта предельного ядра | **Выведена** из фазового хвоста (HK2) и представления без парного шума; конечный безусловный второй момент не утверждается |
| Сохранение геометрии признаков при одном фазовом преобразовании | **Доказано** равенство замкнутых образов (HK18); оно не исключает стационарные негауссовские пространства при G |
| Полная отмеченная симметрия при смене корня | **Вычислена точно** для всего окружения: $(\mathcal R_x)_*(w_x\nu)=\nu$, $(\mathcal R_x)_*\nu=\tau\nu$; предел (MP20) обоснован без неравномерного снятия регуляризации |
| Восстановление гауссовости по взвешенному обращению корневой строки | **Не следует из этого маргинального тождества**: конструкция (MP22) работает для любого ненулевого кругового признака со вторым моментом один; полное окружение при этом не построено |
| Положительный резольвентный баланс корневой связи | **Доказан с конечными ожиданиями**, включая общий случай атома $T=0$; через (PH13)–(PH15) показано, что он не выбирает закон $T$ |
| Обновление произвольного HS-оператора при фиксированных iid-признаках | **Замкнуто алгебраически** по (NG9); в конечном ранге обратная переменная исключает стационарную вероятность, а бесконечный ранг этим аргументом не охвачен |

Для завершения пути через плотности остаётся вывести из исходных условий или G существование уровня $u_N\to\infty$ и фазово-инвариантного приближения $\widetilde\mu_{N,u_N}$ такого, что

$$
\|\mu_{N,u_N}-\widetilde\mu_{N,u_N}\|_{\rm TV}\to0,
\qquad \chi^2(\widetilde\mu_{N,u_N}\mid H_{u_N})=o(u_N^2).
$$

Вместо условия на $\chi^2$ достаточно было бы доказать для такого приближения $D(\widetilde\mu_{N,u_N}\|H_{u_N})=o(\log u_N)$. Это **достаточные**, а не объявленные необходимыми критерии. Их нельзя добавлять к исходным условиям молча. Путь через согласованные семейства требует доказательства их единственности при G; одна абсолютная непрерывность уже исключена как достаточный аргумент. Путь через положительные коэффициенты требует асимптотики на длинах $O(N)$ с сохранением знаковых сокращений; доказанный хвост устраняет только длины намного больше $N$. В якобианном подходе остаётся контроль энтропии слоёв и усиления среднего граничного якобиана.

После исследования степенных проекций остаются **два отдельных незакрытых перехода**. Первый — получить нужную обратную оценку для каждой фиксированной степени $r$, а не только полную явную передачу до степени два. Второй — контролировать интеграл высокого остатка равномерно при росте степени. Даже доказательство первого не было бы доказательством второго. Абстрактная положительная модель из раздела TCG и точные формулы OT показывают, почему простое перемножение хааровской нормы с оценкой фиксированных моментов не завершает этот аргумент.

В пути через оператор Стейна и моменты Лежандра весь фазовый вес уже устранён точно. Тем не менее остаётся вывести из исходных условий исчезновение ограниченных наблюдаемых $\mathcal R_\ell$ на трёхканальном уровне. Это эквивалентный конечномерный остаток, **а не доказанное следствие G**. Наличие большого числа внутренних источников само по себе не даёт нужной условной изотропии.

В положительном частотном представлении G даёт $a_v^{(r)}\sim r!v^{-r}$ и тем самым сведения о мерах $\vartheta_r$ возле $t=1$. Для первого момента нужна вся мера: $\mathbb ET=\int(1-t)\vartheta_1(dt)$. Совместимость степеней существенно усилена точным тождеством смены корня: все законы $\mathcal L_r$ имеют одну общую меру $M$, а искомое среднее равно $\int(1-p_0)p_1\,dM$. Выведены её положительные матричные ограничения и законные дисконтированные суммы. Но сумма без дисконта, которая могла бы казаться законом сохранения, расходится. Более того, отдельный полный набор бесконечных Gram-ограничений и всех одномерных тождеств inner-степени не определяет даже среднее: доказано точное тождество (BG6) и выписано семейство (BG7) с произвольным средним из $(0,1)$. Полная спектральная классификация (SG10) усиливает этот вывод: Gram-положительность эквивалентна неотрицательности сферического преобразования закона. Плотность $2-6T+6T^2$ проходит весь бесконечный критерий, имеет среднее $1/2$ и второй момент $11/30$. Формула (SG19) даёт такие неравномерные плотности с любым заранее заданным конечным числом точных хааровских моментов. Доказательства того, что полная inner-совместимость вместе с G определяет $M$ однозначно, пока нет; свободное изменение её маргиналов не объявлено реализуемым унитарным контрпримером.

В представлении через переменные Коши найдены точные средние всех произведений резольвент с параметрами в одной полуплоскости и характеристическая функция матрицы реакции на положительном конусе. Эти равенства сами по себе не определяют $T$: приведён явный конечномерный пример с одинаковой такой информацией и разными законами пропускания. Для смешанного усреднения формула (CD27) оставляет совместное распределение направления связи и матрицы выхода; его изотропия не выведена. Теорема (XC5) усиливает ограничение одностороннего метода: даже все совместные положительные источники после всех фиксированных левых и правых внешних смешиваний автоматически имеют одну характеристическую функцию при любом глобально фазово-инвариантном законе. Пример, отделяющий эти два вида информации, нарушает условие на $P$ и не объявляется контрпримером основной задаче.

В пути через обменные ядра снята дополнительная гипотеза квадратичной интегрируемости: универсальный фазовый хвост (HK2) и усечённый закон больших чисел дают $F_\Theta\in L^2$ почти наверное, поэтому спектральное разложение с квадрат-суммируемыми коэффициентами теперь обосновано. Однако независимость этих спектральных признаков не выведена. Более того, равенство (HK18) показывает, что фазовое обновление сохраняет всё замкнутое пространство признаков на данной реализации; совместная гауссовость этого пространства не возникает только от смены спектрального базиса. Исключение всех стационарных негауссовских геометрий при полной G остаётся отдельным недоказанным шагом. Полная отмеченная формула (MP9), точное смещение меры (MP17) и обоснованный предел (MP20) уточняют требуемую совместимость: необходимо работать со всем окружением $(a,L)$. Одно взвешенное обращение строки допускает произвольный круговой закон признака по (MP22). Скалярный положительный баланс (PH10) также не добавляет ограничения на закон $T$, как показывает его точное выражение (PH15). Дополнительный класс (NG2)–(NG5) алгебраически замкнут для недиагонального HS-оператора, но ни его бесконечномерная стационарность, ни полная фазовая инвариантность и G не установлены.

Независимые аудиты проверили алгебру, размерности, условия интегрируемости, смену глобального параметра в обменном представлении и применимость привлечённых внешних теорем. Внесены исправления конвенции сопряжения спектральных признаков, явной нормировки LSI на $SU(n)$ и проекционного доказательства для Gram-ядер. В последнем цикле отдельно проверены знаки в формуле взаимного рассеяния, диапазоны размеров конечных примеров, различие фиксированных и фазозависимых симметрий, точный множитель энергетической оценки и граничные случаи матричного ядра Пуассона. Для последнего продолжение точного замыкания на каждый фиксированный граничный параметр и каждую нагрузку без условия обратимости не утверждается; итоговая теорема с независимыми случайными фазами включает границу корректным предельным переходом. Ни одно из этих уточнений не меняет статус основной цели.

Дополнительные независимые проверки этой редакции охватили все формулы второй степени при символической размерности, коэффициенты хааровской матрицы Грама, исходно-взвешенную условную G, знаки поперечного якобианного минора симплектической семьи, точные четырёхканальные интегралы, нормы моментных тензоров и заполнение последовательностей во всех размерах. В последнем продолжении отдельно проверены полярное переплетение оператора Березина, нормировка преобразования Мелера–Фока, полиномиальные спектральные множители, хвостовая оценка для ядра Кэли, сохранение замкнутого образа, полная формула Байеса при смене корня, обоснование предела через лемму Шеффе, точная компенсация квадратичных форм и конечномерный аргумент со случайным блужданием обратного оператора. Проверенные первичные статьи о случайных унитарных схемах используют независимые последовательные слои; их теоремы не подменяют повторное использование одной фазовой диагонали в исходном ансамбле.

Техническая проверка этой редакции охватила 5 779 математических фрагментов: 1 131 отдельную формулу и 4 648 внутристрочных фрагментов. Формулы проверены извлечением в XeLaTeX; компиляция завершилась без синтаксических ошибок и сообщений о недостающих символах. Проверены 431 уникальный формульный тег, все ссылки новых разделов на эти теги, парность математических разделителей и ограждений кода, а также синтаксис трёх включённых Python-блоков. Это проверка целостности и набора записи; доказательства оцениваются по приведённым аргументам и независимым математическим проверкам, а не по успешной компиляции.

В документе нет утверждения, что эти препятствия неразрешимы или что исходная теорема ложна. Есть точная фиксация того, что доказано, что проверено лишь численно, какие предложенные конструкции не работают и какое следствие осталось недоказанным. **Полным решением поставленной задачи этот документ не является: не доказан даже предел первого момента в исходной общности и не предъявлен допустимый контрпример к закону $T_N$.**

# TC-2026-10-17-6: exact phase-averaging, generic escape and quantum Kac identity

**Status of the main problem.** Neither the limit law `T_N => Uniform[0,1]` under the three original assumptions nor an admissible counterexample has been obtained. The results below are rigorous *finite-dimensional* theorems. Unlike the entropy or G-based criteria developed earlier, Theorems AP1--AP4 below require no conditional Gaussian input. The final subsection specifies why they do not imply transmission equipartition.

## AP1. The holomorphic evaluation identity and conditional spectral laws

Let `S in U(N)`, fix a nonempty proper channel set `A` of cardinality `m`, and put `E=A^c`, `r=N-m`. For `Z=diag(z_e:e in E)` define

$$
F_A(Z)=S_{AA}+S_{AE}(I-ZS_{EE})^{-1}ZS_{EA}.
\tag{AP1}
$$

For `|z_e|<1`, the matrix is holomorphic in the `r` variables and is a contraction. Indeed, with external input `x`, internal input `v=Zw_E`, and outgoing amplitudes `(w_A,w_E)=S(x,v)`, unitarity gives

$$
\|x\|^2-\|F_A(Z)x\|^2
=\|w_E\|^2-\|Zw_E\|^2\ge0.
\tag{AP2}
$$

The function extends to an almost-everywhere unitary boundary value on the phase torus. Its constant term is exactly `F_A(0)=S_{AA}`. Repeated Cauchy mean-value integration therefore proves, for *every* holomorphic polynomial `H` in the matrix entries,

$$
\boxed{\quad \mathbb E_Z H(F_A(Z))=H(S_{AA}).\quad}
\tag{AP3}
$$

This holds without flatness, without gaps for `P` and `B`, and without Haar assumptions. It also holds conditional on all `r-1` **relative phases**. More precisely, select `e_0 in E` and write `z_e=t\eta_e`, where `t=z_{e_0}` and `\eta_{e_0}=1`. For independent uniform phases `z_e`, the variable `t` is uniform and independent of the relative phase vector `eta`. For every fixed `eta`,

$$
\boxed{\quad \mathbb E_t H(F_A(t\operatorname{diag}\eta))=H(S_{AA}).\quad}
\tag{AP4}
$$

This stronger *conditional* identity follows by the ordinary one-variable Cauchy formula.

Set `Delta(t,eta)=det F_A(t diag(eta))` and `delta=det S_{AA}`. Since `|Delta|=1` on the boundary and

$$
\mathbb E_t \Delta^k=\delta^k\quad (k\ge1),
\tag{AP5}
$$

the conditional law of `Delta` is **exactly** the scalar Poisson kernel with centre `delta`:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb P(\arg\Delta\in d\theta\mid\eta)
=\frac{1-|\delta|^2}{|e^{i\theta}-\delta|^2}
\frac{d\theta}{2\pi},\qquad |\delta|<1.
\quad}
\tag{AP6}
$$

In particular, the determinant of the output is independent of the entire vector of relative internal phases. The independence here concerns its *conditional probability law*, not pointwise functional dependence. When `|delta|=1`, the distribution is the corresponding Dirac mass.

Let `sigma_{A,eta}` be the conditional expected empirical eigenphase measure of the unitary `F_A(t diag eta)` (normalized by `1/m`). For each `k>=1`,

$$
\int_{\mathbb T} z^k\,\sigma_{A,\eta}(dz)
=\frac1m\operatorname{Tr}(S_{AA}^k).
\tag{AP7}
$$

If `a=||S_{AA}||_{op}<1`, Fourier uniqueness yields a smooth density,

$$
\boxed{\quad
\frac{d\sigma_{A,\eta}}{d\theta}
=\frac1{2\pi m}\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}
\left[(I+e^{-i\theta}S_{AA})(I-e^{-i\theta}S_{AA})^{-1}\right].
\quad}
\tag{AP8}
$$

It is independent of `eta`. Expanding the resolvent and integrating its Fourier series gives

$$
\|\sigma_{A,\eta}-\operatorname{Haar}(\mathbb T)\|_{TV}
\le\frac{a}{1-a},\qquad
\|\mathcal L(\Delta\mid\eta)-\operatorname{Haar}(\mathbb T)\|_{TV}
\le\frac{|\delta|}{1-|\delta|}.
\tag{AP9}
$$

Under the original flatness assumption, uniformly in the choice of `A` of fixed size `m`,

$$
\|S_{AA}\|_{op}\le m\sqrt{C_0/N},\qquad
|\det S_{AA}|\le(m C_0/N)^{m/2}.
\tag{AP10}
$$

Therefore the **one-point eigenphase distribution** is uniform with total-variation error `O_m(N^{-1/2})`, and the determinant phase with error `O_m(N^{-m/2})`. These are genuinely unconditional results (in particular for `m=2`), but they do *not* imply that the eigenphases are jointly distributed as CUE, nor do they identify the off-diagonal intensity `T`.

### AP1a. The exact mixed Bernstein--determinant hierarchy

Now let `m=2`, write `F=F_A`, `T=|F_{12}|^2`, `Delta=det F`, and define the deterministic numbers

$$
u=S_{11}S_{22},\qquad v=-S_{12}S_{21},\qquad \delta=u+v.\tag{AP11}
$$

For any `2x2` unitary `F`, the adjugate identities give

$$
F_{11}F_{22}=(1-T)\Delta,\qquad
-F_{12}F_{21}=T\Delta.
\tag{AP12}
$$

Consequently, for all integers `k,l>=0` and `q>=k+l`, the product on the left below is *holomorphic* in the entries of `F`. Applying (AP4) produces the exact **conditional** formula

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_t\left[T^k(1-T)^l\Delta^q\mid\eta\right]
=v^k u^l\delta^{q-k-l}.
\quad}
\tag{AP13}
$$

For negative exponents the conjugate identities hold. In particular,

$$
\mathbb E_t[T\Delta\mid\eta]=-S_{12}S_{21},\qquad
\mathbb E_t[(1-T)\Delta\mid\eta]=S_{11}S_{22}.
\tag{AP14}
$$

Thus, for a flat input, every mixed Fourier coefficient in (AP13) with `k+l>=1` is `O(N^{-k-l})`. However, the required unweighted moment `E[T^k]` corresponds to `q=0`, *outside* this holomorphic wedge. Taking `Delta^{-k}` in (AP12) is not analytic across interior zeros of `Delta`. This is precisely the boundary obstruction, not a technical omission.

The identities cannot by themselves identify `T`: if `Theta` is uniform and independent of an **arbitrary** `[0,1]`-valued variable `T_0`, then

$$
U=e^{i\Theta}
\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1-T_0}&\sqrt{T_0}\\-\sqrt{T_0}&\sqrt{1-T_0}\end{pmatrix}
\tag{AP15}
$$

has uniform determinant phase, uniform averaged eigenphase measure, and all positive-degree holomorphic moments zero, regardless of the law of `T_0`. This is an abstract countermodel to inferring transmission from those particular moments, **not** an admissible sequence `S_N`.

## AP2. Generic complete escape from every nonempty set of channels

**Theorem (generic no-dark-state theorem).** Let `S in U(N)`. In its directed support graph, draw `j -> i` whenever `S_{ij} != 0`. Assume this graph is strongly connected. Let `A` be any nonempty proper set, `E=A^c`. If `Lambda=diag(eta_e:e in E)` contains independent Haar phases, then

$$
\boxed{\qquad \rho(\Lambda S_{EE})<1\quad\text{almost surely}.\qquad}
\tag{AP16}
$$

No pointwise entry lower bound or random-matrix theorem is required. In the user's original setting, strong connectivity follows from `||P-J||_{op}<1`: a finite doubly stochastic matrix with more than one communicating class has a nonconstant fixed vector orthogonal to constants and hence `||P-J||_{op}>=1`.

**Proof, Part I: each internal coordinate has winding number one.** Fix `e in E` and eliminate every index of `E\setminus{e}` with independent phases. Denote the remaining unitary matrix, indexed by `A union {e}`, by `S'`. The coupling `S'_{ae}` is a rational holomorphic function of the eliminated phase variables. Because the directed support graph is strongly connected, there is a shortest directed path

$$
e=v_0\longrightarrow v_1\longrightarrow\cdots
\longrightarrow v_{L-1}\longrightarrow v_L=a\in A
\tag{AP17}
$$

whose interior vertices are all in `E\setminus{e}`. Expand the exact Schur transfer entry in the open polydisk. The coefficient of the square-free monomial `z_{v_1}...z_{v_{L-1}}` is precisely

$$
S_{a,v_{L-1}}S_{v_{L-1},v_{L-2}}\cdots S_{v_1,e}\ne0.
\tag{AP18}
$$

To check that no alternative ordering cancels it: any path that uses precisely the same interior vertices and has length `L` must also be a shortest path to `A`. Every vertex occurring at position `j` in such a path must have graph distance exactly `j` from `e`; the set in (AP17) has exactly one vertex at each distance `j=1,...,L-1`. Hence the order is unique. For `L=1`, use the nonzero constant term `S_{ae}` instead.

The coupling is therefore not identically zero. Since the defining denominator has constant term `1`, it is nonzero for almost every boundary phase vector. A nonzero analytic rational numerator also vanishes only on a torus set of Haar measure zero. Consequently `S'_{ae} != 0` almost surely. From unitarity,

$$
|S'_{ee}|<1\qquad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{AP19}
$$

Now eliminate `e` with its remaining phase `z_e`. The determinant of the final `m`-port matrix is, by the block determinant lemma,

$$
\det F_A(z_e)=\det S'\,
\frac{\overline{S'_{ee}}-z_e}{1-z_e S'_{ee}}.
\tag{AP20}
$$

This is a degree-one finite Blaschke factor (up to a unimodular constant). Its boundary phase derivative is nonnegative, with conditional integral exactly one:

$$
 h_e(Z):=\frac\partial{\partial\phi_e}\arg\det F_A(Z)
=\frac{1-|S'_{ee}|^2}{|e^{i\phi_e}-\overline{S'_{ee}}|^2},
\qquad
\mathbb E_Z h_e(Z)=1.
\tag{AP21}
$$

Here `S'` depends on every phase *except* `phi_e`, so integration in the latter is legitimate. Notice that the argument uses the **full collection of independently variable phases**; for an exceptional configuration such as all phases equal, dark states may remain.

**Proof, Part II: sum the winding numbers along the common phase.** Write `Z=t Lambda` as in AP1, `t=e^{i\theta}`, and introduce the unitary colligation

$$
W_\Lambda=
\begin{pmatrix}A_0&B_0\\C_0&Q\end{pmatrix}
=
\begin{pmatrix}S_{AA}&S_{AE}\\\Lambda S_{EA}&\Lambda S_{EE}\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{AP22}
$$

The block Schur determinant identity, proved by multiplying the block matrix by `W_Lambda^*`, gives

$$
\boxed{\quad
\det F_A(t\Lambda)=\det W_\Lambda\,
\frac{\det(Q^*-tI_E)}{\det(I_E-tQ)}.
\quad}
\tag{AP23}
$$

Indeed, for
`M(t)=[[A_0,B_0],[-t C_0,I-tQ]]` one finds
`M(t)W_Lambda^*=[[I,0],[B_0^*,Q^*-tI]]`.
Since `||Q||<=1`, any eigenvalue `lambda` of `Q` is in the closed disk. In (AP23) a unimodular eigenvalue contributes a constant factor after cancelling `conj(lambda)-t` against `1-t lambda`. Every strictly interior eigenvalue contributes one Blaschke zero. Therefore

$$
\deg_{\rm Blaschke}\det F_A(t\Lambda)
=d(\Lambda):=\#\{\lambda\in\sigma(Q):|\lambda|<1\},
\qquad 0\le d(\Lambda)\le r,
\tag{AP24}
$$

with algebraic multiplicities.

Using the chain rule and Fubini/Tonelli (all individual derivatives in AP21 are nonnegative), the expected winding in the common phase is

$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E_\Lambda d(\Lambda)
&=\mathbb E_{\Lambda}\frac1{2\pi}
\int_0^{2\pi}\frac\partial{\partial\theta}
\arg\det F_A(e^{i\theta}\Lambda)\,d\theta\\
&=\mathbb E_Z\sum_{e\in E}h_e(Z)
=\sum_{e\in E}1=r.
\end{aligned}
\tag{AP25}
$$

Since the integer `d(Lambda)` is at most `r`, it must equal `r` almost surely. Hence `Q` has no eigenvalues of modulus one, proving (AP16). This completes the proof.

**Corollary AP2a (simple spectrum and nonvanishing eigenvectors).** Under the same strong connectivity assumption, for almost every full diagonal Haar phase matrix `D`, all eigenvalues of `DS` are simple and **every coordinate of every normalized eigenvector is nonzero**. Indeed, apply AP16 with `A={a}`. If `DS psi=lambda psi` and `psi(a)=0`, its restriction to `E` would be a unit-modulus eigenvector of `D_E S_EE`, contradicting AP16. Intersect the resulting probability-one events over the finitely many coordinates `a`. Finally, a repeated eigenvalue would have an eigenvector vanishing at a prescribed coordinate by taking a linear combination in its eigenspace. This proves simplicity.

Thus, for all sufficiently large `N` satisfying the original gap assumption, the zero-denominator convention in the source-weighted ratio measure is unnecessary: all `w_{li}>0` almost surely. Equivalently, every coordinate vector is a cyclic vector for `DS`, almost surely (simple spectrum plus nonzero spectral projection at every eigenvalue).

## AP3. An exact quantum Kac identity at the Heisenberg scale

The preceding theorem leads to a sharp *conservation identity*, not merely a reduction. Keep `m=|A|` fixed and set `r=N-m`, with the matrices `A_0,B_0,C_0,Q` in (AP22). Expand the matrix-valued inner function in the common phase:

$$
F_A(t\Lambda)=F_0+\sum_{k\ge1}t^k F_k,
\qquad
F_0=A_0,
\qquad F_k=B_0 Q^{k-1}C_0.
\tag{AP26}
$$

Because `rho(Q)<1` almost surely under AP2, this expansion converges past the closed unit disk for almost all `Lambda`. Unitarity of `W_Lambda` gives the two exact identities

$$
Q Q^*+C_0C_0^*=I_E,
\qquad Q^*Q+B_0^*B_0=I_E.
\tag{AP27}
$$

First, telescope the former relation:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\|Q^k C_0\|_F^2
&=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\operatorname{Tr}
\bigl[Q^k(I-QQ^*)(Q^*)^k\bigr]\\
&=r-\operatorname{Tr}[Q^K(Q^*)^K].
\end{aligned}
\tag{AP28}
$$

Since `rho(Q)<1`, the last trace tends to zero. From the second identity, with `a_k=||Q^kC_0||_F^2`,

$$
\|F_{k+1}\|_F^2=a_k-a_{k+1}.
\tag{AP29}
$$

Summation by parts now gives the **exact deterministic-for-almost-every-phase pair of identities**

$$
\boxed{\quad
\sum_{k\ge0}\|Q^k C_0\|_F^2
=\sum_{k\ge1}k\|F_k\|_F^2
=N-m.
\quad}
\tag{AP30}
$$

Independently, unitary boundary values and ordinary Parseval give

$$
\boxed{\quad
\sum_{k\ge0}\|F_k\|_F^2=m,
\qquad
\sum_{k\ge1}\|F_k\|_F^2=m-\|S_{AA}\|_F^2.
\quad}
\tag{AP31}
$$

Define `p_k^{(b)}=sum_{a in A}|(F_k)_{ab}|^2` for input `b in A`. By AP31 each `p^{(b)}` is a genuine probability distribution on nonnegative integers `k` (with `k=0` denoting immediate exit). AP30 proves the sharp quantum Kac law

$$
\boxed{\quad
\frac1m\sum_{b\in A}\sum_{k\ge0} k p_k^{(b)}
=\frac{N-m}{m}\quad\text{for almost every relative phase vector}.\quad}
\tag{AP32}
$$

For `m=2`, the total mean output delay is *exactly* `(N-2)/2`. This identity holds for each sufficiently large `N` in the original problem and does not use the cooperon gap `||B||<1`. It rigorously identifies the Heisenberg (`k=O(N)`) scale in the channel-summed delay. It does **not** imply an `O(N)` median, tail concentration, or equal probability of exiting through the two detectors.

For a general unitary input without irreducibility, the same proof holds with `N-m` replaced by the number of eigenvalues of `Q` strictly inside the disk; the complementary unitary reducing subspace contributes no observable escape. This is the exact boundary case of (AP30).

### AP3a. Positive first-moment representation and the remaining imbalance

For `m=2`, denote input channel 2 by `b` and output channel 1 by `a`. Ordinary Parseval yields, for every fixed relative phase vector,

$$
\mathbb E_t T\mid\Lambda
=|S_{ab}|^2+
\sum_{k\ge1}|(B_0Q^{k-1}C_0)_{ab}|^2.
\tag{AP33}
$$

This is the exact integrated quantum *absorption probability into detector `a`*. The same formula for the second detector sums, together with (AP33), to one. Hence the original first-moment goal is exactly a **balanced-exit statement** for a quenched diagonal-phase quantum contraction,

$$
\mathbb E_\Lambda\left[|S_{ab}|^2+
\sum_{k\ge1}|(B_0Q^{k-1}C_0)_{ab}|^2\right]
\longrightarrow\frac12.
\tag{AP34}
$$

AP30 controls only the *sum over inputs and detectors of the first temporal moment*; it does not control the detector imbalance in AP34. For example, if `S` is a single directed-cycle permutation, then the support graph is strongly connected, AP16 and AP30 hold exactly, and `B=0`, but the feedback scattering between any two retained sites is an off-diagonal monomial unitary and `T=1` for every set of phases. This example violates both original flatness and the `P-J` operator gap (`||P-J||=1`), so it is not a counterexample to the main problem. It proves sharply why complete escape and the Kac identity alone do not entail the uniform transmission law.

## AP4. Rigorous short-time leakage bounds and the exact two-step cooperon term

These results use the original flatness but not the conditional input G. Assume `max_{ij}|S_{ij}|^2 <= C_0/N`. In the Fourier expansion (AP26), for fixed `a,b in A` and `k>=1`, each scalar coefficient is the phase sum over ordered internal paths with `k` visited vertices:

$$
(F_k)_{ab}
=\sum_{e_1,\ldots,e_k\in E}
S_{a,e_k}S_{e_k,e_{k-1}}\cdots S_{e_1,b}
\eta_{e_1}\cdots\eta_{e_k}.
\tag{AP35}
$$

A full set of independent phases can be used for evaluating its squared modulus: fixing one relative phase only multiplies `F_k` by a common unimodular factor. Path pairs survive integration only when their vertex-occupation multisets coincide. A fixed ordered word has at most `k!` partner words with the same multiset. Cauchy--Schwarz *within each multiset* therefore gives

$$
\mathbb E_\Lambda |(F_k)_{ab}|^2
\le k!\sum_{e_1,\ldots,e_k\in E}
P_{a,e_k}P_{e_k,e_{k-1}}\cdots P_{e_1,b}.
\tag{AP36}
$$

The classical sum on the right is at most `C_0/N`: use `P_{a,e_k}<=C_0/N` and apply the column-substochastic property of the restricted matrix `P_{EE}` repeatedly. Thus

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_\Lambda |(F_k)_{ab}|^2\le\frac{C_0 k!}{N},
\qquad k\ge1.
\quad}
\tag{AP37}
$$

Including `F_0=S_{AA}`, for fixed `m` and

$$
L_N=\left\lfloor(1-\varepsilon)\frac{\log N}{\log\log N}\right\rfloor,
\qquad0<\varepsilon<1,
\tag{AP38}
$$

we have

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_\Lambda\sum_{k=0}^{L_N}\|F_k\|_F^2
\le \frac{m^2C_0}{N}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{L_N}k!\right)
=N^{-\varepsilon+o(1)}\longrightarrow0.
\quad}
\tag{AP39}
$$

This rigorously rules out substantial output-energy concentration at bounded or logarithmic-length paths. It remains far from the `k=O(N)` sector required by AP34; indeed `k!` makes AP37 ineffective there. The earlier estimates for the high-length tail and AP30 quantify the opposite end of this same open time window.

For `k=2` one can expose exactly how the original `B` enters the first nontrivial interference correction. Write `B^{\rm coop}_{ef}=S_{ef}\overline{S_{fe}}` (to distinguish this matrix from the coupling block `B_0`) and, for fixed `a,b in A`, let

$$
u_e=S_{ae}\overline{S_{eb}},\qquad e\in E.
\tag{AP40}
$$

Pairing the orderings `(e,f)` and `(f,e)` of the same two internal phases gives

$$
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E_\Lambda |(F_2)_{ab}|^2
={}&\sum_{e,f\in E}P_{ae}P_{ef}P_{fb}\\
&+\langle\overline u,B^{\rm coop}_{EE}\overline u\rangle
-\sum_{e\in E}P_{ee}|u_e|^2.
\end{aligned}}
\tag{AP41}
$$

The subtracted diagonal term prevents double-counting pairs with `e=f`. Since `||u||_2^2=sum_e P_{ae}P_{eb}<=C_0/N`, the original cooperon bound implies

$$
\boxed{\quad
0\le\mathbb E_\Lambda |(F_2)_{ab}|^2
\le\frac{C_0(1+\rho_*)}{N}.
\quad}
\tag{AP42}
$$

This provides an exact, rigorously bounded appearance of the third hypothesis in a physically relevant interference coefficient. It is *not* an induction for the sector `k=O(N)`: at `k>=3`, different permutations of paths produce new correlated loops not determined by the single matrix `B^{coop}`. Those terms remain unbounded at the required scale by this argument.

## AP5. Computational audit and negative-control experiments

The following calculations are independent reproducibility checks, **not** inputs to AP1--AP4 and not proofs of convergence. Scripts in the same downloadable research bundle are named `tc_verify_exact.py`, `tc_shorttime_check.py`, `tc_numeric.py`, `tc_tensor.py`.

* For a fixed seven-channel Haar input and for the `7 x 7` Fourier matrix, five internal channels and random relative phases, the finite sums approximating both sides of AP30 gave `4.999999999999977` and `5.000000000000015`, respectively. Their independently evaluated survival sums were `4.999999999999990` and `5.000000000000011`. Conditional integrals of `det F` and `T det F` agreed with AP5 and AP14 to about `10^{-16}`.
* For a seven-channel Haar input, a direct Monte Carlo evaluation of the `k=2` coefficient intensity yielded `0.0583354 +/- 0.0001647` (one standard error), while AP41 predicts `0.05850116` exactly.
* For the explicit symplectic flat unitaries with `N=9` and `N=81`, Monte Carlo averages of `T` were `0.5190` (2000 samples) and `0.4896` (550 samples); the corresponding second moments were `0.3622` and `0.3201`. Their cooperon norms were `1/3` and `1/9`. These experiments reveal no violation of the conjectured limit.
* Fourier inputs, which violate the third hypothesis with `||B||=1`, gave means about `0.4045` (`N=9`, 2000 samples) and `0.3461` (`N=81`, 550 samples). The difference is a diagnostic, not a quantitative theorem for Fourier sequences.
* Two admissible tensor-Hadamard families have **exactly** `P=J`, with fixed cooperon norms approximately `0.72361` (`5 x 5` base) and `0.54313` (`7 x 7` base). Sample means at dimensions `N=105,155` (first family) and `N=77,147` (second family) were near `0.5`. These are exploratory data, not uniform asymptotic bounds.
* A directed-cycle permutation with `N=11` had `T=1`, `rho(Q)=0`, `B=0` and `||P-J||=1`. It verifies that even maximal escape and zero cooperon norm do not replace the classical mixing hypothesis.

## AP6. Exact boundary of the new results

1. AP2 proves that, under the original classical operator gap, phase feedback has **no dark internal states almost surely**, and it also proves generic simple spectrum and full eigenvector support. These resolve the finite-dimensional spectral regularity assumptions that were previously carried as genericity statements.
2. AP3 gives an **equality** for the full channel-summed first temporal moment, rather than only the upper bound used in the previous length-tail argument. The total escape scale is exactly `(N-m)/m` averaged over incoming channels. No conjectural approximation is involved.
3. AP1 gives conditional one-point spectral universality, scalar determinant Poisson laws and infinitely many exact mixed determinant/transmission moments. These do **not** fix the unweighted balanced moments of `T` because the interior zeros of `det F` obstruct holomorphic division.
4. AP4 controls paths of length up to `(1-eps)log N/loglog N`, and identifies the exact two-step cooperon contribution, but does **not** control the critical `O(N)`-length path sector. AP2--AP4 are compatible with the directed-cycle countermodel when flatness or the classical gap is removed.
5. **Still missing:** A quantitative, nonperturbative channel-equipartition estimate for the quenched quantum absorption Gramian in AP34, *uniform over arbitrary deterministic* `S_N` satisfying all three original hypotheses; equivalently, a valid argument for `E T_N^k ->1/(k+1)` for all fixed `k`. The present theorems solve neither this step nor the full two-channel conjecture. No admissible counterexample was found.

The new proofs use no hidden Haar-eigenvector assumption, no unproved transfer of local Gaussian convergence to boundary resolvents, and no unregulated interchange of the spectral or phase limits. The only interchange of infinite nonnegative sums is by Tonelli; the rational one-variable expansions converge beyond the unit circle after AP2.

## AP7. Self-contained verification script

The exact formulae can be checked without any external research framework. This script is deliberately small and checks the essential symbolic identities numerically without claiming a numerical proof of the asymptotic conjecture:

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.linalg import solve

rng = np.random.default_rng(12877)

def haar(n):
    X = rng.normal(size=(n,n)) + 1j*rng.normal(size=(n,n))
    Q,R = np.linalg.qr(X)
    return Q @ np.diag(np.diag(R).conj()/abs(np.diag(R)))

def feedback(S,A,phases):
    E = [i for i in range(len(S)) if i not in A]
    SAA=S[np.ix_(A,A)]; SAE=S[np.ix_(A,E)]
    SEA=S[np.ix_(E,A)]; SEE=S[np.ix_(E,E)]
    X = solve(np.eye(len(E)) - phases[:,None]*SEE,
              phases[:,None]*SEA)
    return SAA+SAE@X

n=7; A=[0,3]; E=[i for i in range(n) if i not in A]
S=haar(n); lam=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n-2))
Q=lam[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,E)]
C=lam[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,A)]
B=S[np.ix_(A,E)]
assert max(abs(np.linalg.eigvals(Q))) < 1

# Formula AP30: weighted outgoing probability = internal dimension.
value=0.; X=C.copy()
for k in range(20000):
    value+=(k+1)*np.linalg.norm(B@X)**2
    X=Q@X
    if np.linalg.norm(X)<1e-9: break
assert abs(value-(n-len(A))) < 1e-7

# Conditional scalar Poisson and Bernstein identities.
t=np.exp(2j*np.pi*np.arange(16384)/16384)
F=np.array([feedback(S,A,w*lam) for w in t])
dets=np.linalg.det(F)
T=np.abs(F[:,0,1])**2
SAA=S[np.ix_(A,A)]
assert abs(np.mean(dets)-np.linalg.det(SAA))<1e-8
assert abs(np.mean(T*dets)+SAA[0,1]*SAA[1,0])<1e-8
print('All exact finite-dimensional checks passed.')
```

The script verifies the predicted values, not an asymptotic limit. Deterministic tests for the `k=2` occupation-permutation identity and the original-model candidate families were also run; their summary appears in AP5.

# TC-2026-10-17-7: spectral averaging, bounded fractional overlaps, Mahler determinants and sharp exceptional scars

**Status.** These are additional finite-dimensional identities, a new equivalent bounded fractional-moment criterion, a sharpened controllability identity, and one exact construction limiting deterministic delocalization claims. They are not a proof of the required asymptotic two-channel law and not an admissible probabilistic counterexample. All identities below refer to the original phase model, not a Haar replacement. The external quantum-graph theorem is discussed only as a checked possible route and is not invoked as an applicable theorem.

## SA1. Exact rank-one spectral averaging with the source coordinate fixed

Let `S in U(N)` be deterministic. Let `D=diag(z_1,...,z_N)` have independent uniform unit-circle variables, and put `U=DS`. For a fixed index `i`, the spectral probability measure at `e_i` is

$$
\mu_i^U=\sum_{\ell=1}^N w_{\ell i}\,\delta_{\lambda_\ell},\qquad
w_{\ell i}=|\psi_\ell(i)|^2.
\tag{SA1}
$$

Fix all the phases except `z_i`. Then **exactly**, without sending `N` to infinity,

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_{z_i}\mu_i^U(d\lambda)=\frac{d\theta}{2\pi},
\quad \lambda=e^{i\theta}.
\quad}
\tag{SA2}
$$

**Proof.** For every positive integer `k`, the diagonal entry `(U^k)_{ii}` is a finite path sum. Every monomial contains at least one factor `z_i`: the first row factor in the product is `z_i S_{i,*}`. There are no negative powers of phases in `U^k`. Hence `E_{z_i}(U^k)_{ii}=0` for all `k>=1`, regardless of all other phases. For negative `k`, the diagonal entries are complex conjugates of the corresponding positive-power diagonal entries because `U` is unitary. Thus all nonzero Fourier coefficients of the averaged spectral measure vanish; its mass is one. Density of trigonometric polynomials in the continuous periodic functions proves (SA2). No assumption about level spacings or Gaussian eigenvectors is used. QED.

If an eigenvalue is degenerate, formulate (SA1) using the spectral projection, so (SA2) remains valid. Under the original `||P-J||<1`, the previously established complete-escape theorem implies simplicity and `w_{\ell i}>0` almost surely.

Choose a random eigenvalue index with conditional probability `w_{\ell i}`. By (SA2), **the chosen eigenphase is uniform and independent of all other phases**. This statement concerns the size-biased spectral selection; the ordinary, unweighted empirical eigenvalue process is not asserted to be independent of the cavity.

Let `E=[N]\setminus{i}` and fix `j in E`. The eigenvalue equation at `\lambda` gives

$$
\frac{\psi_\ell|_E}{\psi_\ell(i)}
= (\lambda I_E-D_E S_{EE})^{-1}D_E S_{Ei}.
\tag{SA3}
$$

In the source-weighted selection, `\lambda` is independent uniform on the circle. Multiplying all cavity phases by `\lambda^{-1}` preserves their joint distribution. Therefore, for every bounded Borel `f:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb R`,

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_D\sum_\ell w_{\ell i}
  f\!\left(\frac{w_{\ell j}}{w_{\ell i}}\right)
=
\mathbb E_{D_E}f\!\left(
\left|\left[(I_E-D_E S_{EE})^{-1}D_E S_{Ei}\right]_j\right|^2
\right).
\quad}
\tag{SA4}
$$

Ratios with a zero source weight are defined arbitrarily and contribute zero; the original hypotheses exclude them almost surely. The resolvent exists almost surely by the previous complete-escape theorem. Equation (SA4) turns a weighted sum over *all* eigenvectors into a **single fixed-energy one-port cavity resolvent**, with no spectral regularization. It does not yet control its distribution.

## SA2. Recover the exact two-port transform and all fractional Mellin observables

Eliminate `E\setminus{j}` first. The remaining unitary two-port scattering matrix, in the order `(i,j)`, is `F` with transmission `T=|F_{ji}|^2` and reflection `d=F_{jj}`. Close channel `j` by a further independent uniform `z`. The internal amplitude from source `i` at `j` is

$$
 g_j=\frac{z F_{ji}}{1-z F_{jj}},\qquad
 R:=|g_j|^2=\frac{T}{|1-zd|^2},\qquad |d|^2=1-T.
\tag{SA5}
$$

After an inessential phase shift of `z`,

$$
R\ \stackrel d=\ \frac{T}{1+(1-T)-2\sqrt{1-T}\cos\Phi},
\qquad
Y=\frac{R}{1+R}=\frac{T}{2(1-\sqrt{1-T}\cos\Phi)}.
\tag{SA6}
$$

This is an independent verification of the exact spectral-ratio/scattering transform. A useful additional fact is that, whenever `T>0`,

$$
\mathbb E_z(R\mid T)=\frac{T}{1-|d|^2}=1.
\tag{SA7}
$$

In particular, `E R=1` for every fixed ordered source/detector pair in the connected model. This is also immediate from `\sum_\ell w_{\ell j}=1` after source bias, and is **not** a Haar-universality statement.

For `0<s<1`, the angular integral is exactly

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathcal H_s(t):=
\mathbb E_\Phi\left[\left.
\left(\frac{t}{|1-\sqrt{1-t}e^{i\Phi}|^2}\right)^s
\right|t\right]
=t^s\,{}_2F_1(s,s;1;1-t).
\quad}
\tag{SA8}
$$

Set `H_s(0)=0`, `H_s(1)=1`. The hypergeometric series for `0<t<=1` follows by multiplying the binomial expansions of `(1-rz)^{-s}` and `(1-r\bar z)^{-s}` and retaining equal exponents. For `0<s<1`, `\mathcal H_s` is continuous on `[0,1]` and takes values in `[0,1]`: conditional on any `t>0`, `E R=1`, so concavity yields `E R^s<=1`. To check continuity at `t=0`, observe `R_t\to0` for every `\Phi\ne0`; the family `R_t^s` is uniformly integrable because `E[R_t^s 1_{R_t^s>K}]<=K^{(s-1)/s} E R_t`.

The Euler transformation gives an **exact pointwise identity**

$$
\boxed{\quad \mathcal H_s(t)=\mathcal H_{1-s}(t).\quad}
\tag{SA9}
$$

Thus the size-biased Mellin symmetry alone imposes no restriction on `T`; it is already valid for **each deterministic** `t`. This prevents mistaking a formal moment symmetry for a universality proof.

## FM1. A denominator-free, bounded and complete equivalent criterion

For every `0<s<1`, define the fractional eigenfunction overlap

$$
\boxed{\quad
 M_N(s)=\frac1{N(N-1)}\,
\mathbb E_D\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\sum_{i\ne j}
  w_{\ell i}^{\,1-s} w_{\ell j}^{\,s}.
\quad}
\tag{FM1}
$$

All summands are nonnegative and finite. Since `\sum_i w_{\ell i}=1`, Hoelder's inequality gives `0<=M_N(s)<=1`, and exchanging `(i,j)` gives `M_N(s)=M_N(1-s)`. In contrast to the original ratio measure, (FM1) contains **no division by a small eigenvector coordinate**.

Using (SA4)--(SA8) and uniform choice of the ordered pair,

$$
\boxed{\quad M_N(s)=\mathbb E\,\mathcal H_s(T_N).\quad}
\tag{FM2}
$$

For the target law `T\sim Uniform[0,1]`, the original source-weighted ratio is `Y\sim Beta(1,2)`. Therefore `R=Y/(1-Y)` has the **exact** beta-prime density

$$
 f_R(r)=\frac{2}{(1+r)^3}\mathbf 1_{r>0},
\tag{FM3}
$$

with Mellin transform

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E R^s=\Gamma(1+s)\Gamma(2-s),
\qquad -1<s<2.
\quad}
\tag{FM4}
$$

**Theorem FM1 (complete bounded criterion).** The desired transmission universality is equivalent to

$$
\boxed{\quad
T_N\Rightarrow Uniform[0,1]
\ \Longleftrightarrow\ 
M_N(s)\longrightarrow\Gamma(1+s)\Gamma(2-s)
\quad\text{for every }0<s<1.
\quad}
\tag{FM5}
$$

Indeed, the forward direction follows from weak convergence and continuity of `\mathcal H_s`. For the converse, extract any weakly convergent subsequence of the laws of `T_N`, possible by compactness of `[0,1]`, and call its limit `\mu`. The associated mixture law of `R` has the target Mellin transform for all `s in (0,1)`. This Mellin transform is holomorphic in the strip `0<Re(s)<1`: on each compact substrip the necessary logarithmic factors are controlled by neighbouring moments, while `E R^a<=1` for `a in [0,1]`. For any fixed `c in (0,1)` it determines the Fourier transform of the finite measure `e^{cx}Law(\log R)(dx)` and therefore uniquely determines the law of `R` on `(0,\infty)`. Its total mass is the beta-prime target; hence an atom at zero is impossible. Consequently `R` has density (FM3), `Y=R/(1+R)` is `Beta(1,2)`, and injectivity of the given two-channel transform forces `\mu=Uniform[0,1]`. All subsequential limits are identical. QED.

In fact it suffices to know convergence in (FM5) on **any fixed set of real `s` with an accumulation point inside `(0,1)`**, by the holomorphic identity theorem.

The benchmark is exact at every finite `N` when the eigenvector matrix is Haar. A Haar eigenvector's intensity vector has Dirichlet parameters `(1,...,1)`, hence

$$
\mathbb E\bigl[w_i^{1-s}w_j^s\bigr]
=\frac{\Gamma(2-s)\Gamma(1+s)}N
\quad(i\ne j),
\tag{FM6}
$$

and `M_N(s)` equals (FM4) **without a finite-N error**. This is only a check of the target; no Haar property is assumed for the deterministic input.

For `s=1/2`, the criterion is a particularly simple nonlinear eigenvector statistic:

$$
\boxed{\quad
M_N(1/2)=\frac{1}{N(N-1)}\mathbb E_D
\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\left[
\left(\sum_{i=1}^N|\psi_\ell(i)|\right)^2-1
\right].
\quad}
\tag{FM7}
$$

The Haar target is `\pi/4`, whereas a completely flat eigenbasis would give `1`. A real-Gaussian intensity benchmark (the orthogonal symmetry class) gives `2/\pi`. Equivalently,

$$
\mathcal H_{1/2}(t)=\frac{2\sqrt t}{\pi}
K\!\left(\sqrt{1-t}\right),
\tag{FM8}
$$

where `K(k)` is the complete elliptic integral of the first kind with modulus `k`. The target `M_N(1/2)\to\pi/4` is **necessary but by itself not sufficient** for the full theorem.

The nontrivial missing step is to control these *fractional* overlaps for the actual random-phase eigenvectors at `N`-scale. The supplied Gaussian input G concerns large-channel Schur blocks; it has not been shown to control (FM1).

## UC1. A strengthened exact matrix Kac/controllability law

In the notation of the preceding appendix, form the unitary colligation

$$
W=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\C&Q\end{pmatrix},
\qquad \rho(Q)<1.
\tag{UC1}
$$

Unitarity gives `CC^*+QQ^*=I_E` and `B^*B+Q^*Q=I_E`. Iterating these two identities, **as operator equalities**, yields

$$
\boxed{\quad
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}Q^kCC^*(Q^*)^k=I_E,
\qquad
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(Q^*)^kB^*BQ^k=I_E.
\quad}
\tag{UC2}
$$

Both series telescope and converge in operator norm because `\rho(Q)<1` in finite dimension. This is strictly stronger than the previously derived *trace* equality of total Heisenberg-scale delay. In entries, the time-and-port vectors form exact Parseval frames:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\sum_{b\in A}\sum_{k\ge0}
(Q^k C)_{e b}\,\overline{(Q^k C)_{f b}}
=\delta_{ef},\qquad e,f\in E.
\quad}
\tag{UC3}
$$

There is a dual identity with `(BQ^k)_{ae}` summed over output ports `a` and time. Consequently every internal state is completely reachable and observable over all times through the open channels, with total Gramian precisely the identity. This does **not** say that the contributions from the *individual* two open channels are each half the identity.

There is also an exact positive *matrix-valued* Wigner--Smith identity. With

$$
F(z)=A+z B(I-zQ)^{-1}C,
\tag{UC4}
$$

unitarity and direct block multiplication imply the de Branges--Rovnyak identity

$$
\boxed{\quad
I-F(z)^*F(w)
=(1-\bar z w)\,C^*(I-\bar z Q^*)^{-1}(I-wQ)^{-1}C.
\quad}
\tag{UC5}
$$

Taking a radial boundary derivative at `z=w=e^{i\theta}` gives

$$
\boxed{\quad
-iF(e^{i\theta})^*\frac{d}{d\theta}F(e^{i\theta})
=C^*(I-e^{-i\theta}Q^*)^{-1}
(I-e^{i\theta}Q)^{-1}C\ \succeq0.
\quad}
\tag{UC6}
$$

For a one-port model, its right side is the total internal norm `\sum_{j\ne i}|g_j(e^{i\theta})|^2`. Integrating its trace over the common phase recovers `N-m` by (UC2). This is an exact positive *nonregularized* identity, but it does not provide detector equipartition.

## MM1. A determinantal/Mahler formula for the logarithmic transmission

Fix the ordered distinct external indices `(a,b)` and `E=[N]\setminus\{a,b\}`. Write `Z=diag(z_e:e in E)`, and define the multi-affine polynomials

$$
\begin{aligned}
p(Z)&=\det(I_E-ZS_{EE}),\\
q_{ab}(Z)&=\det\begin{pmatrix}
 S_{ab}&S_{aE}\\-ZS_{Eb}&I_E-ZS_{EE}
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{MM1}
$$

The Schur determinant formula gives `F_{ab}(Z)=q_{ab}(Z)/p(Z)`. Expanding in the diagonal phase monomials `z_K=\prod_{e\in K}z_e` gives **exact minor formulae**

$$
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
p(Z)&=\sum_{K\subseteq E}(-1)^{|K|}\det S_{K,K}\ z_K,\\
q_{ab}(Z)&=\sum_{K\subseteq E}(-1)^{|K|}
\det S_{[a,K],[b,K]}\ z_K.
\end{aligned}}
\tag{MM2}
$$

In the second line `a` is placed first in the chosen row order, `b` first in the column order, and the remaining elements of `K` have the same fixed order in both. For the empty set, `\det S_{\varnothing,\varnothing}=1`.

The denominator `p` is zero-free on the open unit polydisk and satisfies `p(0)=1`, because `\|ZS_{EE}\|<1` when all `|z_e|<1`. Multivariable Jensen applied to the holomorphic logarithm of `p`, followed by the standard radial boundary limit for a nonzero polynomial, yields its (logarithmic) Mahler measure

$$
 m(p):=\int_{\mathbb T^E}\log|p(Z)|\,dZ=0.
\tag{MM3}
$$

Under the original connectivity gap, the transmission coefficient is nonzero almost surely; `q_{ab}` is not the zero polynomial. Logarithms of the moduli of nonzero trigonometric polynomials are integrable over the torus, so the following quantity is **finite**:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_{D_E}\log T_{ab}
=2m(q_{ab})
=2\int_{\mathbb T^E}\log|q_{ab}(Z)|\,dZ.
\quad}
\tag{MM4}
$$

No resolvent moment or boundary cutoff appears. For any multi-affine polynomial `q(Z)=\sum_Kc_Kz_K`, iterative one-variable Jensen (`\int\log|a+bz|=\log\max\{|a|,|b|\}`) gives `m(q)>=\log |c_K|` for **every** corner coefficient. Therefore

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E\log T_{ab}\ \ge\
2\log\max_{K\subseteq E}
\left|\det S_{[a,K],[b,K]}\right|.
\quad}
\tag{MM5}
$$

This is a rigorous but currently far-too-weak lower bound: when `P=J`, the empty coefficient yields only `-\log N`, whereas the target `Uniform[0,1]` has `E\log T=-1`.

Jacobi's complementary-minor theorem for unitary matrices gives a further exact identity. If `c_{ab,K}=(-1)^{|K|}\det S_{[a,K],[b,K]}`, then

$$
\boxed{\quad
c_{ab,K}=(-1)^{N+1}\det S\,\overline{c_{ba,E\setminus K}}.
\quad}
\tag{MM6}
$$

Equivalently, for all torus phases,

$$
\boxed{\quad
q_{ab}(Z)=(-1)^{N+1}\det S\,
\left(\prod_{e\in E}z_e\right)
\overline{q_{ba}(Z)}.
\quad}
\tag{MM7}
$$

This polynomial-level reciprocity implies `|q_{ab}(Z)|=|q_{ba}(Z)|`, hence equality of the two directed transmission intensities, and matches the physical two-port unitarity identity. It imposes no random-matrix law on the remaining internal Fourier coefficients.

The expectation in (MM4) may be expressed directly through the weighted eigenvector intensities by differentiating (FM1) at `s=0`:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E\log T_N
=\frac1{N(N-1)}\,
\mathbb E_D\sum_{\ell=1}^N
\left[\sum_{j=1}^N\log w_{\ell j}
-N\sum_{i=1}^Nw_{\ell i}\log w_{\ell i}\right].
\quad}
\tag{MM8}
$$

This equality is understood for fixed `N` under the original strong-connectivity assumption, ensuring the finite logarithmic integrals. It is an exact entropy/logarithmic-volume representation, not a proof that its right side tends to `-1`.

## EN1. Deterministic entropic uncertainty and a sharp exceptional countermodel

The input flatness gives `c:=\max_{ij}|S_{ij}|\le\sqrt{C_0/N}`. If `DS\psi=\lambda\psi`, then `|(S\psi)_j|=|\psi_j|` coordinatewise, because `D` and `\lambda` are unimodular. The standard entropic uncertainty inequality for a unitary matrix (which follows by Riesz--Thorin interpolation between `\ell^1\to\ell^\infty` with bound `c` and `\ell^2\to\ell^2` with bound `1`, then differentiating at `p=q=2`) gives

$$
H(|\psi|^2)+H(|S\psi|^2)\ge-2\log c.
\tag{EN1}
$$

Thus every eigenvector of **every** phase realization satisfies

$$
\boxed{\quad
H(|\psi|^2):=-\sum_j|\psi_j|^2\log|\psi_j|^2
\ge\frac12\log(N/C_0).
\quad}
\tag{EN2}
$$

For a subset `L` with `|L|=k<=N/2` and eigenvector mass at least `1-\delta` inside `L`, where `0<=\delta<1/2`, the entropy decomposition between `L` and its complement yields

$$
H(|\psi|^2)\le h(\delta)+(1-\delta)\log k+\delta\log N,
\quad
h(\delta)=-\delta\log\delta-(1-\delta)\log(1-\delta).
\tag{EN3}
$$

Combining these bounds,

$$
\boxed{\quad
 k\ge C_0^{-1/[2(1-\delta)]}
 e^{-h(\delta)/(1-\delta)}
 N^{(1/2-\delta)/(1-\delta)}.
\quad}
\tag{EN4}
$$

The exponent `1/2` for exact support is **optimal even with all three original hypotheses and vanishing cooperon norm**, at exceptional (measure-zero) phase configurations. Here is a concrete construction.

Take `q=3^d`, `N=q^2` and indices `x=(a,b)` with `a,b in \mathbb F_3^d`. Define the symplectic character table

$$
S_{(a,b),(c,e)}=\frac1q\exp\!\left(
\frac{2\pi i}{3}(a\cdot e-b\cdot c)
\right).
\tag{EN5}
$$

Orthogonality of finite-field characters gives `S=S^*=S^{-1}`, `P=J`, `C_0=1`, and

$$
\boxed{\quad \|B\|_{\rm op}=1/q=N^{-1/2}.\quad}
\tag{EN6}
$$

Indeed `B_{xy}=N^{-1}\exp(4\pi i\langle x,Jy\rangle/3)` is `q^{-1}` times another unitary character transform (the factor `2` is invertible in `\mathbb F_3`). Choose

$$
\psi_{(a,b)}=q^{-1/2}\mathbf 1_{\{b=0\}}.
\tag{EN7}
$$

The character sum over all `a` gives `S\psi=\psi` **exactly**. Therefore for every diagonal `D` satisfying `D_{(a,0)}=1` for all `a`, with arbitrary phases elsewhere, `DS\psi=\psi`. This eigenvector has precisely `q=\sqrt N` nonzero coordinates and

$$
H(|\psi|^2)=\log q=\tfrac12\log N,
\tag{EN8}
$$

so (EN2) is attained with equality. The phases fixed on the support have joint Haar probability zero. This construction is **not** a counterexample to the original random-phase statement, and (EN8) says nothing about typical phases. It proves that no deterministic per-phase improvement of the exponent `1/2` can follow solely from the three input matrix inequalities. Any full proof must genuinely exploit typical phase randomness.

## QG1. A direct quantum-star-graph representation and a precise literature obstruction

The original setup can be realized as scattering on a star graph with one central vertex having scattering matrix `S`, `N` peripheral one-bond vertices and two opened leads. An unopened peripheral bond is returned with a phase `z_e`; a chosen lead is perfectly coupled. Eliminating the peripheral bonds gives exactly `F_A(Z)`, up to harmless propagation phases of the open leads. The `N` independent phases can be generated by averaging the wave number along bounded rationally independent bond lengths.

The classical Perron--Frobenius operator on the `2N` **directed** star bonds has the form (up to a permutation of the two directional blocks)

$$
\mathscr P=\begin{pmatrix}0&P\\I&0\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
\mathscr P^2=\begin{pmatrix}P&0\\0&P\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{QG1}
$$

In particular it has the **two exact peripheral eigenvalues `+1` and `-1`**, even when `P=J`. All its remaining eigenvalues have modulus at most `1/10` under `||P-J||op<=1/100`, since the nontrivial spectrum of `P` has modulus at most `1/100`.

Pluha\v{r} and Weidenmueller's paper *Quantum Graphs and Random-Matrix Theory* (2015, arXiv:1407.1148, https://arxiv.org/abs/1407.1148) states a fixed-channel random-matrix scattering result under classical **mixing** assumptions that exclude any other unit-modulus Perron--Frobenius eigenvalue. Therefore its hypotheses do **not** literally hold for this star realization. Formally the `-1` mode has quadratic mass `1-(-1)=2`; proving that it can be discarded in the complete **unregularized, fixed-channel** generating function requires a new uniform argument, not merely the observation of a positive quadratic mass. The published analysis estimates terms in a massive-mode loop expansion separately; that does not by itself furnish the uniform remainder needed in the present problem. Consequently this publication is a possible technical source, **not** a solved black-box reduction.

The transformed stroboscopic classical dynamics on just the central-bond sector is `P`, which does satisfy the original gap; developing a rigorously controlled phase-integral formulation on that reduced sector remains an identified research avenue.

## V7. Independent numerical/algebraic verification, and what remains

The supporting scripts saved in the working bundle are:

- `tc_spectral_cavity_check.py`: verifies `SA4` by independently sampling size-biased eigenvector ratios and single-source cavity ratios at `N=5`; mean bounded ratio `0.43959` versus `0.44431` with 4000 samples, within Monte Carlo error, and the weighted eigenphase first Fourier coefficient is about `0.00458`.
- `tc_fractional_mellin.py`: quadrature verifies `int_0^1 H_s(t)dt=Gamma(1+s)Gamma(2-s)` for `s=0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9` to approximately `10^{-12}`. It also confirms the pointwise `s <-> 1-s` identity.
- `tc_fractional_numeric.py`: at `N=81`, 330 samples of each family, the reciprocal/elliptic overlap `M(1/2)` is approximately `0.6314 +/- 0.0150` for the symmetric Fourier matrix (`||B||=1`, excluded by the main theorem) and `0.7818 +/- 0.0110` for the admissible symplectic family (`||B||=1/9`); compare the orthogonal benchmark `2/pi=0.6366` and the Haar target `pi/4=0.7854`. These are **not** asymptotic proofs.
- `tc_mahler_check.py` and `tc_minor_torus.py`: compare the determinant-minor expansions and Jacobi duality for randomly generated unitaries of orders 3--8, with errors about `10^{-15}`.
- `tc_mahler_numeric.py`: Monte Carlo verifies `E log|p|^2=0` and `E log T=2m(q)` for a nontrivial six-channel sample.
- `tc_entropy_sharp.py`: at `N=81`, verifies `||S^*S-I||F` about `1.1e-14`, `||S psi-psi||2` about `4e-16`, `||B||=1/9`, and **exact** support `9=sqrt(81)` with equality in the entropy bound.

**Exact unresolved target.** None of (SA1)--(EN8), the star realization, or the independently verified computations proves

$$
\mathbb ET_N^k\to\frac1{k+1},\quad k\ge1,
\tag{OPEN7}
$$

or exhibits an admissible counterexample. In the new representation it is enough (and necessary) to show `M_N(s)->Gamma(1+s)Gamma(2-s)` on any fixed real set with an interior accumulation point. This is a bounded, denominator-free observables program and therefore avoids one genuine analytic difficulty. However the currently available gap and Gaussian-Schur hypotheses have not been shown to control those observables. The exact symplectic sparse eigenvector demonstrates that deterministic entropy bounds cannot replace the missing **probabilistic** estimate.

No result in this appendix is labelled a full solution. Do not infer a Haar law from the cited graph paper, from finite-dimensional algebraic tests, from the conditional input G, or from the existence of exceptional phase vectors.

## V7b. Embedded reproducibility scripts (complete source)

The following source files are embedded here so that the present Markdown is self-contained. They do not replace proofs.

### Source: `tc_appendix7_verify.py`

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.linalg import solve
from scipy.special import hyp2f1, gamma
from itertools import combinations

rng=np.random.default_rng(24401)
n=6
X=rng.normal(size=(n,n))+1j*rng.normal(size=(n,n))
Q,R=np.linalg.qr(X)
S=Q@np.diag(np.conj(np.diag(R))/abs(np.diag(R)))
i,j=1,4
E=[e for e in range(n) if e!=i]
eidx=E.index(j)
ze=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n-1))
# Deterministic quadrature in the single source phase. Fourier moments < grid length integrate exactly.
grid=720
fourier=np.zeros(5, dtype=complex)
mellinA=0.0
s=.37
for t in range(grid):
 z=np.ones(n,complex)
 z[E]=ze
 z[i]=np.exp(2j*np.pi*t/grid)
 U=z[:,None]*S
 ev,v=np.linalg.eig(U)
 w=np.abs(v[i,:])**2
 wj=np.abs(v[j,:])**2
 for k in range(1,len(fourier)+1):
  fourier[k-1]+=np.sum(w*ev**k)/grid
 mellinA+=np.sum(w**(1-s)*wj**s)/grid
# Conditional measure in theta is uniform: compute cavity ratio directly by quadrature in the spectral angle.
mellinB=0.
for t in range(grid):
 lam=np.exp(2j*np.pi*t/grid)
 g=solve(lam*np.eye(n-1)-ze[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,E)], ze*S[E,i],check_finite=False)
 mellinB+=abs(g[eidx])**(2*s)/grid
print('rank-one Fourier modes',np.array2string(fourier,precision=3))
print('fixed-cavity weighted Mellin lhs rhs difference',mellinA,mellinB,mellinA-mellinB)

# Confirm finite-N torus averaged Mellin vs two-port hypergeometric transform.
Nsim=4000
left=[]; right=[]
for run in range(Nsim):
 z=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n))
 U=z[:,None]*S
 _,v=np.linalg.eig(U)
 w=np.abs(v)**2
 left.append(sum(np.sum(w[i,:]**(1-s)*w[j,:]**s) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i!=j)/(n*(n-1)))
 a,b=rng.choice(n,2,replace=False)
 EE=[e for e in range(n) if e not in (a,b)]
 z2=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n-2))
 K=np.eye(n-2)-z2[:,None]*S[np.ix_(EE,EE)]
 f=S[a,b]+S[a,EE]@solve(K,z2*S[EE,b],check_finite=False)
 T=abs(f)**2
 right.append(T**s*hyp2f1(s,s,1,1-T))
print('all-index fractional overlap vs two-port H_s',np.mean(left),np.mean(right),'samplingSE',np.std(left,ddof=1)/np.sqrt(Nsim),np.std(right,ddof=1)/np.sqrt(Nsim))
print('Haar fractional benchmark',gamma(1+s)*gamma(2-s))
```

### Source: `tc_entropy_sharp.py`

```python
import numpy as np
p=3;d=2;q=p**d;N=q*q
coords=np.indices((p,)*d).reshape(d,-1).T
A=np.repeat(coords,q,axis=0);B=np.tile(coords,(q,1))
J=(A@B.T-B@A.T)%p
S=np.exp(2j*np.pi*J/p)/q
w=np.all(B==0,axis=1)
psi=np.zeros(N,dtype=complex);psi[w]=1/np.sqrt(q)
print('N=',N,'support=',w.sum(),'sqrt(N)=',np.sqrt(N))
print('unitarity Fro err',np.linalg.norm(S@S.conj().T-np.eye(N)))
print('Spsi-psi',np.linalg.norm(S@psi-psi))
Bcoop=S*np.conj(S.T)
print('B norm',np.linalg.norm(Bcoop,2),'target',1/np.sqrt(N))
print('entropy',-np.sum(abs(psi[w])**2*np.log(abs(psi[w])**2)),'target',0.5*np.log(N))
```

### Source: `tc_minor_torus.py`

```python
import numpy as np
rng=np.random.default_rng(13)
for n in (4,5,6):
 x=rng.normal(size=(n,n))+1j*rng.normal(size=(n,n));u,r=np.linalg.qr(x);S=u@np.diag(np.conj(np.diag(r))/abs(np.diag(r)))
 a,b=0,n-1;E=[i for i in range(n) if i not in (a,b)]; z=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(len(E)))
 M=np.eye(len(E))-z[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,E)]
 def numerator(a,b):
  return np.linalg.det(np.block([[np.array([[S[a,b]]]),S[np.ix_([a],E)]],[-z[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,[b])], M]]))
 lhs=numerator(a,b);rhs=(-1)**(n+1)*np.linalg.det(S)*np.prod(z)*np.conj(numerator(b,a))
 print(n,abs(lhs-rhs))
```

```python
import numpy as np
from itertools import combinations
from scipy.linalg import solve
rng=np.random.default_rng(531)
for n in [4,6,8]:
 x=rng.normal(size=(n,n))+1j*rng.normal(size=(n,n))
 q,r=np.linalg.qr(x);S=q@np.diag(np.diag(r).conj()/abs(np.diag(r)))
 a,b=1,0;E=[i for i in range(n) if i not in (a,b)];z=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n-2))
 d=np.linalg.det(np.eye(n-2)-z[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,E)])
 f=S[a,b]+S[np.ix_([a],E)]@solve(np.eye(n-2)-z[:,None]*S[np.ix_(E,E)],z*S[np.ix_(E,[b])][:,0])
 num=0j; den=0j
 for k in range(n-1):
  for subset in combinations(range(n-2),k):
   K=[E[i] for i in subset];prod=np.prod(z[list(subset)])
   num+=(-1)**k* np.linalg.det(S[np.ix_([a]+K,[b]+K)])*prod
   den+=(-1)**k*(np.linalg.det(S[np.ix_(K,K)]) if k else 1)*prod
 print(n,abs(num-d*f.item()),abs(den-d),abs(f.item()),abs(num),flush=True)
```

The rigorous new statements are SA1--SA9, FM1--FM8, UC1--UC6, MM1--MM8, and EN1--EN8, with QG1 recording a precise non-applicability of a potentially relevant published graph theorem. # TC-2026-10-17-8: the exact Hardy-model-space structure and the multivariate Student benchmark

**Status.** The following are proved, exact finite-dimensional structural statements for the original diagonal-phase/Schur ensemble and an independent calculation of the Haar benchmark. They do **not** establish the original two-port transmission limit under the three input assumptions. No Haar law is assigned to the actual output. The statements sharpen the new rank-one spectral-averaging approach (SA1--SA8) by identifying its full functional-analytic structure and the precise law a successful universality proof would have to reproduce.

## HS1. Fixed-relative-phase exact orthonormality of all cavity Green coordinates

Fix a source channel `i`, put `E=[N]\setminus\{i\}`, `d=N-1`, and fix an arbitrary internal phase vector `\eta=(\eta_e)_{e\in E}` for which the contraction `Q=D_\eta S_{EE}` has spectral radius `<1`. This occurs almost surely for **every** source `i` under the original classical spectral-gap assumption, as proved in the preceding appendices. Define

$$
W=\operatorname{diag}(1,D_\eta)S
=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\c&Q\end{pmatrix}\in U(d+1),
\qquad
c=D_\eta S_{Ei},\quad a=S_{ii},\quad b=S_{iE},
\tag{HS1}
$$

and the scalar characteristic function and vector of analytic functions

$$
F(z)=a+z b(I-zQ)^{-1}c,\qquad
h(z)=(I-zQ)^{-1}c\in\mathbb C^d.
\tag{HS2}
$$

They are analytic on a neighbourhood of the closed unit disk, because `\rho(Q)<1` (no bound on `\|Q\|` less than one is needed). Unitarity of `W` gives the **exact de Branges--Rovnyak / unitary-colligation identity**

$$
\boxed{\quad
\frac{1-\overline{F(z)}F(w)}{1-\bar zw}
 =h(z)^*h(w)=\sum_{j\in E}\overline{h_j(z)}h_j(w),
\qquad z,w\in\mathbb D.
\quad}
\tag{HS3}
$$

Here and below `*` means conjugate transpose. To verify (HS3), expand the transfer function and use the block-unitarity relations `1-|a|^2=c^*c`, `a^*b+c^*Q=0`, `b^*b+Q^*Q=I`, or equivalently the standard unitary resolvent identity; all denominators are invertible on the unit disk.

The stronger statement is the *matrix orthogonality law*, valid with all the relative phases frozen:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\int_0^{2\pi}h(e^{i\theta})h(e^{i\theta})^*\,\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}
=I_d.
\quad}
\tag{HS4}
$$

**Proof.** Expand the stable transfer resolvent,

$$
h(e^{i\theta})=\sum_{k\ge0}e^{ik\theta}Q^kc.
$$

Unitarity gives `cc^*=I_d-QQ^*` because the lower block row `[c,Q]` is coisometric. By Parseval,

$$
\begin{aligned}
\int hh^*\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}
&=\sum_{k\ge0}Q^kcc^*(Q^*)^k\\
&=\sum_{k\ge0}\bigl(Q^k(Q^*)^k-Q^{k+1}(Q^*)^{k+1}\bigr)\\
&=I_d-\lim_{K\to\infty}Q^{K+1}(Q^*)^{K+1}=I_d,
\end{aligned}
$$

where the last limit holds because `\rho(Q)<1`. QED.

Under the simultaneous common rotation `D_E=e^{i\theta}D_\eta`, the cavity Green vector at spectral energy `1` is

$$
g(e^{i\theta})
=(I-e^{i\theta}Q)^{-1}e^{i\theta}c
=e^{i\theta}h(e^{i\theta}),
\tag{HS5}
$$

hence its covariance is also the identity for **each fixed relative-phase orbit**:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_{\theta}\left[g_j(e^{i\theta})\overline{g_k(e^{i\theta})}\mid\eta\right]
=\delta_{jk},\quad j,k\in E.
\quad}
\tag{HS6}
$$

As a consequence the *unconditional* covariance of the vector in the rank-one spectral-averaging identity (SA4) is `I_d`, with no appeal to the large-N mixing or cooperon gaps. This is consistent with, and strictly stronger in conditioning than, the one-coordinate equality `\mathbb E R=1` following from the two-port transform. It must not be misinterpreted as independence or Gaussianity of the coordinates. Their joint fourth moments can diverge.

## HS2. An exact Hardy-model-space basis and the Blaschke-phase speed

Because `W` is unitary, `F` is a scalar rational inner function. Since `\rho(Q)<1`, it is a finite Blaschke product of degree exactly `d`: (HS4) gives `d` orthonormal functions in the model space, and (HS3) identifies its reproducing kernel. More explicitly, set

$$
K_F:=H^2(\mathbb D)\ominus F H^2(\mathbb D).
$$

The kernel of `K_F` is `K_F(w,z)=(1-F(w)\overline{F(z)})/(1-w\bar z)`, so by (HS3), its kernel is exactly the sum of the rank-one kernels generated by the components `h_j`. Formula (HS4) says these components are orthonormal in the Hardy space `H^2`; hence

$$
\boxed{\quad
\{h_j(\cdot):j\in E\}\text{ is an orthonormal basis of }K_F,
\qquad \dim K_F=d=N-1.
\quad}
\tag{HS7}
$$

This identifies the entire one-port cavity Green family with a concrete orthonormal rational-function system, not a collection of independently sampled complex Gaussians.

On the boundary, the angular derivative of the Blaschke phase is the squared norm of that Green vector:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\frac{d}{d\theta}\arg F(e^{i\theta})
=\|h(e^{i\theta})\|^2
=\|g(e^{i\theta})\|^2\ge0.
\quad}
\tag{HS8}
$$

**Proof.** In (HS3), let `z=w=re^{i\theta}` and `r\uparrow1`. The left side tends to `\overline{F(e^{i\theta})}e^{i\theta}F'(e^{i\theta})` by the boundary Julia derivative identity (or one application of l'Hopital to the explicit rational product); this equals the real nonnegative angular derivative. The right side tends to `\|h(e^{i\theta})\|^2`. Integrating the angular derivative gives the degree `d`, in agreement with the trace of (HS4). QED.

This is a *pointwise exact* relation between Wigner--Smith delay, a Blaschke winding, and the full cavity Green norm, with no statistical ansatz. What is missing for universal two-channel transmission is a theorem describing the **direction and non-Gaussian joint distribution** of the components of this orthonormal family when the internal phases vary.

## HS3. Exact Haar target: a multivariate complex Student field

The following calculation is **only a benchmark**, not a law asserted for the deterministic phase ensemble. Suppose a unitary matrix were Haar and choose an eigenvector with probability proportional to its intensity on the source coordinate `i`. For a Haar eigenbasis, if `\psi\in\mathbb C^N` is uniform on the unit sphere, this picked eigenvector has the `N|\psi_i|^2`-size-biased spherical law. Write `u=(\psi_j/\psi_i)_{j\ne i}\in\mathbb C^{d}`, `d=N-1`. Under the unweighted sphere law its complex Cauchy density is

$$
 f_{0,d}(u)=\frac{d!}{\pi^d}(1+\|u\|^2)^{-(d+1)}.
$$

Since `|\psi_i|^2=(1+\|u\|^2)^{-1}` exactly, source-weighting gives the **normalized density**

$$
\boxed{\quad
 f_{d}^{\mathrm{Haar,src}}(u)
 =\frac{(d+1)!}{\pi^d}(1+\|u\|^2)^{-(d+2)},
\qquad u\in\mathbb C^d.
\quad}
\tag{HS9}
$$

It is normalized because `\int_{\mathbb C^d}(1+\|u\|^2)^{-(d+2)}du=\pi^d/\Gamma(d+2)`. A fixed `m`-coordinate marginal, `m\le d`, has the same family with `d` replaced by `m`:

$$
\boxed{\quad
 f_{m}(z_1,\ldots,z_m)
 =\frac{(m+1)!}{\pi^m}
 (1+\textstyle\sum_{j=1}^m|z_j|^2)^{-(m+2)}.
\quad}
\tag{HS10}
$$

Equivalently this entire consistent field has the **exact Gaussian scale-mixture representation**

$$
\boxed{\quad
 u_j=\frac{G_j}{\sqrt V},\qquad
 G_j\stackrel{\rm iid}{\sim}{\rm CN}(0,1),\qquad
 V\sim\operatorname{Gamma}(2,1),\qquad G\perp V.
\quad}
\tag{HS11}
$$

Indeed, conditional on `V=v`, the density is `(v/\pi)^m e^{-v\|u\|^2}` and integration against `v e^{-v}dv` reproduces (HS10). This is a *complex Student t* field with four real degrees of freedom. It is **not** jointly Gaussian: all coordinates share the same random scale `V^{-1/2}`. For a single coordinate,

$$
 f_1(z)=\frac2{\pi(1+|z|^2)^3},\qquad
 \mathbb P\{|u_1|^2\in dr\}=\frac{2\,dr}{(1+r)^3},
\tag{HS12}
$$

which agrees exactly with the beta-prime transform used in FM1. Its fractional moments are

$$
 \mathbb E|u_1|^{2s}=\Gamma(1+s)\Gamma(2-s),\quad-1<s<2.
\tag{HS13}
$$

The scale-mixture also yields the **bounded joint characteristic-function target** for any fixed number `m` of coordinates and any `t\in\mathbb C^m`, with `\langle t,u\rangle=\sum_j\bar t_j u_j`:

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E\exp\bigl(i\operatorname{Re}\langle t,u\rangle\bigr)
=\frac{\|t\|^2}{2}K_2(\|t\|),
\quad \text{with value }1\text{ at }t=0.
\quad}
\tag{HS14}
$$

Here `K_2` denotes the modified Bessel function of the second kind. Formula (HS14) follows from the Gamma integral `\int_0^\infty v e^{-v-b/v}dv=2bK_2(2\sqrt b)` with `b=\|t\|^2/4`.

Thus an explicit (strictly stronger than needed) program would be to prove that the *actual*, source-weighted cavity Green coordinates for uniformly sampled distinct channels converge jointly to the field (HS11). The scalar target can alternatively be obtained by proving convergence of just the radial marginal in (HS12); by the problem's injective transform, this radial condition is equivalent to the required `T_N\Rightarrow U[0,1]`.

## HS4. Why second moments alone cannot select this law; exact higher Mellin identities

The covariance of (HS11) is `\mathbb E u_j\bar u_k=\delta_{jk}` because `\mathbb E[V^{-1}]=1` for `V\sim\operatorname{Gamma}(2,1)`. Formula (HS6) shows that this **same covariance identity holds for the actual phase model at every finite N**, even after fixing all relative internal phases and averaging only their common rotation. Hence covariance gives no universality information.

For integer `r\ge1`, the two-port transform yields a precise comparison with higher radial moments. Writing `R=|g_j|^2`, and conditioning on a two-port transmission `T`, for `T>0` and `\Phi\sim U[0,2\pi]` independent,

$$
 R=\frac{T}{|1-\sqrt{1-T}\,e^{i\Phi}|^2},
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\Phi}R^r
=T^{1-r}\sum_{k=0}^{r-1}\binom{r-1}{k}^2(1-T)^k.
\tag{HS15}
$$

The integer identity is Euler's transformation of `{}_2F_1(r,r;1;1-T)` into the finite polynomial `{}_2F_1(1-r,1-r;1;1-T)`; it holds for every fixed `T>0`. In particular,

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E_{\Phi}R=1,
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\Phi}R^2=\frac{2-T}{T},
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\Phi}R^3=\frac{6-6T+T^2}{T^2}.
\quad}
\tag{HS16}
$$

Because `T>0` a.s. under the initial gap assumptions (by the earlier full-support/analyticity proof), averaging is valid with extended nonnegative expectations. It yields

$$
\boxed{\quad
\mathbb E R^2=2\,\mathbb E[T^{-1}]-1
\quad}\tag{HS17}
$$

with `+\infty` permitted on either side. The Student target (HS12) has `\mathbb E R^2=+\infty`; therefore a proof based on uniformly bounded fourth moments of the unregularized cavity Green field cannot be valid in the proposed universal regime. The *bounded fractional* moments in FM1 avoid precisely this obstruction.

**Research boundary.** The exact Hardy-model-space structure, the conditional isotropy (HS6), and the explicit Student benchmark do **not** prove Gaussian scale-mixture convergence. The missing theorem would have to control the shared random scale and the angular dependence of resolvent coordinates at a fixed boundary energy, despite arbitrarily long coherent returns; the classical and cooperon gaps have not been shown to supply that control. The three original assumptions remain untouched, no admissible nonuniform `T` counterexample has been found, and the full two-channel universality claim remains unproved.

**Independent numerical check.** Script `tc_hardy_exact.py`, also reproduced below, tested (HS3) and (HS4) for QR-sampled unitary matrices at N=3,5,8 and verified errors `3.4e-16`, `2.3e-16`, `1.2e-16` in the kernel and `1.5e-14`, `1.6e-14`, `5.0e-15` in the Lyapunov/Hardy Gramian, respectively. Direct sampling of the Student scale mixture with 300,000 samples gave `E\sqrt R\simeq0.7829` versus exact target `\pi/4\simeq0.7854`, within Monte Carlo precision. Numerical tests are diagnostics, not proofs.

### Verification script (complete)

```python
import numpy as np
from numpy.linalg import norm, solve
from scipy.linalg import solve_discrete_lyapunov
rng=np.random.default_rng(10801)
for n in (3,5,8):
 X=rng.normal(size=(n,n))+1j*rng.normal(size=(n,n))
 U,R=np.linalg.qr(X)
 S=U@np.diag(np.conj(np.diag(R))/np.abs(np.diag(R)))
 i=0
 EE=list(range(1,n))
 zz=np.exp(2j*np.pi*rng.random(n-1))
 Q=zz[:,None]*S[np.ix_(EE,EE)]
 c=zz*S[EE,i]
 b=S[i,EE]
 a=S[i,i]
 F=lambda z: a+z*b@solve(np.eye(n-1)-z*Q,c)
 h=lambda z: solve(np.eye(n-1)-z*Q,c)
 W=solve_discrete_lyapunov(Q,np.outer(c,np.conj(c)))
 z=0.21-0.14j; w=-0.33+0.09j
 kernel=(1-np.conj(F(z))*F(w))/(1-np.conj(z)*w)
 gram=np.vdot(h(z),h(w))
 print(n,'rho Q',max(abs(np.linalg.eigvals(Q))),'gramerr',norm(W-np.eye(n-1)), 'kernelerr',abs(kernel-gram),flush=True)
# Haar target Student field sampling / char compare
m=3;M=300000
V=rng.gamma(shape=2.,scale=1.,size=M)
Z=(rng.normal(size=(M,m))+1j*rng.normal(size=(M,m)))/np.sqrt(2.)
g=Z/np.sqrt(V[:,None])
R=abs(g[:,0])**2
print('Haar-source Student radial bounded moment .5',np.mean(np.sqrt(R)),'target',np.pi/4)
print('joint marginal MSE numerical median',np.mean(R),'limit expectation 1')
```
